3.2 Application Configuration
yaml
# application.yml
spring:
  application:
    name: resilience-demo
  
  # Cache configuration for fallback
  cache:
    type: redis
    redis:
      time-to-live: 300000
      cache-null-values: false

# Resilience4j Circuit Breaker Configuration
resilience4j:
  circuitbreaker:
    configs:
      default:
        register-health-indicator: true
        sliding-window-size: 10
        sliding-window-type: COUNT_BASED
        minimum-number-of-calls: 5
        permitted-number-of-calls-in-half-open-state: 3
        automatic-transition-from-open-to-half-open-enabled: true
        wait-duration-in-open-state: 60s
        failure-rate-threshold: 50
        event-consumer-buffer-size: 10
        record-exceptions:
          - org.springframework.web.client.HttpServerErrorException
          - java.util.concurrent.TimeoutException
          - java.io.IOException
        ignore-exceptions:
          - com.example.resilience.exception.BusinessException
      high-volume:
        register-health-indicator: true
        sliding-window-size: 100
        sliding-window-type: TIME_BASED
        minimum-number-of-calls: 20
        permitted-number-of-calls-in-half-open-state: 10
        wait-duration-in-open-state: 30s
        failure-rate-threshold: 30
      critical:
        register-health-indicator: true
        sliding-window-size: 5
        minimum-number-of-calls: 3
        wait-duration-in-open-state: 120s
        failure-rate-threshold: 40
    instances:
      user-service:
        base-config: default
      order-service:
        base-config: high-volume
      payment-service:
        base-config: critical
      product-service:
        register-health-indicator: true
        sliding-window-size: 20
        minimum-number-of-calls: 10
        permitted-number-of-calls-in-half-open-state: 5
        wait-duration-in-open-state: 45s
        failure-rate-threshold: 45

# Thread pool configuration for bulkhead
resilience4j:
  thread-pool-bulkhead:
    instances:
      order-service-bulkhead:
        max-thread-pool-size: 10
        core-thread-pool-size: 5
        queue-capacity: 20
        keep-alive-duration: 20ms
        writable-stack-trace-enabled: true

# Retry configuration
resilience4j:
  retry:
    instances:
      user-service-retry:
        max-attempts: 3
        wait-duration: 1s
        retry-exceptions:
          - org.springframework.web.client.HttpServerErrorException
          - java.io.IOException

# Time limiter configuration
resilience4j:
  timelimiter:
    instances:
      order-service-limiter:
        timeout-duration: 5s
        cancel-running-future: true

# Management endpoints
management:
  endpoints:
    web:
      exposure:
        include: health,info,metrics,prometheus,circuitbreakers
  endpoint:
    health:
      show-details: always
      show-components: always
    circuitbreakers:
      enabled: true
  metrics:
    export:
      prometheus:
        enabled: true
3.3 Service Implementation with Circuit Breaker
java
package com.example.resilience.service;

import com.example.resilience.model.User;
import com.example.resilience.model.Order;
import com.example.resilience.model.Product;
import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.annotation.CircuitBreaker;
import io.github.resilience4j.retry.annotation.Retry;
import io.github.resilience4j.timelimiter.annotation.TimeLimiter;
import io.github.resilience4j.bulkhead.annotation.Bulkhead;
import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.cache.annotation.Cacheable;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Service;
import org.springframework.web.client.RestTemplate;

import java.util.concurrent.CompletableFuture;
import java.util.concurrent.TimeUnit;

@Slf4j
@Service
public class UserService {
    
    @Autowired
    private RestTemplate restTemplate;
    
    @Autowired
    private FallbackService fallbackService;
    
    /**
     * Basic Circuit Breaker Example
     * Demonstrates CLOSED -> OPEN -> HALF_OPEN -> CLOSED state transitions
     */
    @CircuitBreaker(name = "user-service", fallbackMethod = "getUserFallback")
    public User getUser(String userId) {
        log.info("Fetching user with ID: {}", userId);
        
        // Simulate downstream service call
        String url = "http://user-service/api/users/" + userId;
        User user = restTemplate.getForObject(url, User.class);
        
        // Simulate random failures for demonstration
        simulateRandomFailure();
        
        return user;
    }
    
    /**
     * Fallback method with different strategies based on circuit state
     */
    private User getUserFallback(String userId, Exception ex) {
        log.warn("Circuit breaker OPEN or service failed for user: {}. Using fallback. Error: {}", 
            userId, ex.getMessage());
        
        // Different fallback strategies based on exception type
        if (ex instanceof org.springframework.web.client.HttpServerErrorException) {
            // Server error - return stale cache if available
            return getCachedUser(userId);
        } else if (ex instanceof java.util.concurrent.TimeoutException) {
            // Timeout - return default user
            return getDefaultUser(userId);
        } else {
            // Other errors - return basic fallback
            return fallbackService.getFallbackUser(userId);
        }
    }
    
    /**
     * Cached fallback for better performance
     */
    @Cacheable(value = "users", key = "#userId")
    private User getCachedUser(String userId) {
        log.info("Returning cached user for: {}", userId);
        return User.builder()
            .id(userId)
            .username("cached_" + userId)
            .email("cached@example.com")
            .status("CACHED_FALLBACK")
            .build();
    }
    
    private User getDefaultUser(String userId) {
        return User.builder()
            .id(userId)
            .username("default_user")
            .email("default@example.com")
            .status("DEFAULT_FALLBACK")
            .build();
    }
    
    /**
     * Combined patterns: Circuit Breaker + Retry + TimeLimiter
     */
    @CircuitBreaker(name = "order-service")
    @Retry(name = "order-service-retry")
    @TimeLimiter(name = "order-service-limiter")
    @Bulkhead(name = "order-service-bulkhead")
    public CompletableFuture<Order> getOrderWithProtection(String orderId) {
        log.info("Fetching order with protections: {}", orderId);
        
        return CompletableFuture.supplyAsync(() -> {
            // Simulate slow external service
            try {
                TimeUnit.SECONDS.sleep(2);
            } catch (InterruptedException e) {
                Thread.currentThread().interrupt();
            }
            
            String url = "http://order-service/api/orders/" + orderId;
            return restTemplate.getForObject(url, Order.class);
        });
    }
    
    /**
     * Advanced circuit breaker with custom configuration
     */
    @CircuitBreaker(name = "product-service", fallbackMethod = "getProductFallback")
    public Product getProduct(String productId, boolean criticalOperation) {
        log.info("Fetching product: {} (critical: {})", productId, criticalOperation);
        
        String url = "http://product-service/api/products/" + productId;
        Product product = restTemplate.getForObject(url, Product.class);
        
        // Simulate different failure scenarios
        if (criticalOperation && product.getStockQuantity() == 0) {
            throw new RuntimeException("Product out of stock");
        }
        
        return product;
    }
    
    /**
     * Context-aware fallback with different strategies
     */
    private Product getProductFallback(String productId, boolean criticalOperation, Exception ex) {
        log.error("Fallback triggered for product: {} (critical: {}), error: {}", 
            productId, criticalOperation, ex.getMessage());
        
        if (criticalOperation) {
            // For critical operations, try alternative source
            return getProductFromAlternativeSource(productId);
        } else {
            // For non-critical, return default
            return Product.builder()
                .id(productId)
                .name("Product unavailable")
                .price(0.0)
                .stockQuantity(0)
                .status("UNAVAILABLE")
                .build();
        }
    }
    
    private Product getProductFromAlternativeSource(String productId) {
        // Try backup database or cache
        log.info("Fetching product from alternative source: {}", productId);
        return Product.builder()
            .id(productId)
            .name("Product from backup")
            .price(99.99)
            .stockQuantity(1)
            .status("BACKUP_FALLBACK")
            .build();
    }
    
    /**
     * Simulate random failures to demonstrate circuit breaker transitions
     */
    private void simulateRandomFailure() {
        double random = Math.random();
        
        if (random < 0.3) { // 30% failure rate
            if (random < 0.15) {
                throw new RuntimeException("Simulated service failure");
            } else {
                throw new java.util.concurrent.TimeoutException("Simulated timeout");
            }
        }
    }
    
    /**
     * Reset circuit breaker (for testing/admin purposes)
     */
    public void resetCircuitBreaker(String serviceName) {
        log.info("Manually resetting circuit breaker for: {}", serviceName);
        // Implementation depends on registry access
    }
}
3.4 Custom Circuit Breaker with Event Listeners
java
package com.example.resilience.config;

import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.CircuitBreaker;
import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.CircuitBreakerConfig;
import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.CircuitBreakerRegistry;
import io.github.resilience4j.core.registry.EntryAddedEvent;
import io.github.resilience4j.core.registry.EntryRemovedEvent;
import io.github.resilience4j.core.registry.EntryReplacedEvent;
import io.github.resilience4j.core.registry.RegistryEventConsumer;
import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
import org.springframework.context.annotation.Bean;
import org.springframework.context.annotation.Configuration;

import java.time.Duration;

@Slf4j
@Configuration
public class CustomCircuitBreakerConfig {
    
    @Bean
    public CircuitBreakerRegistry circuitBreakerRegistry() {
        CircuitBreakerConfig config = CircuitBreakerConfig.custom()
            .failureRateThreshold(50)
            .waitDurationInOpenState(Duration.ofSeconds(60))
            .permittedNumberOfCallsInHalfOpenState(3)
            .slidingWindowSize(10)
            .build();
        
        // Create registry with event consumer
        CircuitBreakerRegistry registry = CircuitBreakerRegistry.of(config, 
            createRegistryEventConsumer());
        
        // Add custom event listener to all circuit breakers
        registry.getAllCircuitBreakers().forEach(this::addCustomEventListener);
        
        return registry;
    }
    
    private RegistryEventConsumer<CircuitBreaker> createRegistryEventConsumer() {
        return new RegistryEventConsumer<CircuitBreaker>() {
            @Override
            public void onEntryAdded(EntryAddedEvent<CircuitBreaker> entryAddedEvent) {
                CircuitBreaker circuitBreaker = entryAddedEvent.getAddedEntry();
                log.info("Circuit Breaker added: {}", circuitBreaker.getName());
                addCustomEventListener(circuitBreaker);
            }
            
            @Override
            public void onEntryRemoved(EntryRemovedEvent<CircuitBreaker> entryRemoveEvent) {
                log.info("Circuit Breaker removed: {}", entryRemoveEvent.getRemovedEntry().getName());
            }
            
            @Override
            public void onEntryReplaced(EntryReplacedEvent<CircuitBreaker> entryReplacedEvent) {
                log.info("Circuit Breaker replaced: {}", entryReplacedEvent.getOldEntry().getName());
            }
        };
    }
    
    private void addCustomEventListener(CircuitBreaker circuitBreaker) {
        circuitBreaker.getEventPublisher()
            .onStateTransition(event -> {
                log.info("Circuit Breaker [{}] State Transition: {} -> {}", 
                    circuitBreaker.getName(),
                    event.getStateTransition().getFromState(),
                    event.getStateTransition().getToState());
                
                // Send alert for critical transitions
                if (event.getStateTransition().getToState() == CircuitBreaker.State.OPEN) {
                    sendAlert(circuitBreaker.getName(), "Circuit opened due to failures");
                } else if (event.getStateTransition().getToState() == CircuitBreaker.State.CLOSED &&
                           event.getStateTransition().getFromState() == CircuitBreaker.State.HALF_OPEN) {
                    sendAlert(circuitBreaker.getName(), "Circuit closed after successful recovery");
                }
            })
            .onFailureRateExceeded(event -> {
                log.warn("Circuit Breaker [{}] Failure rate exceeded: {}%", 
                    circuitBreaker.getName(), event.getFailureRate());
            })
            .onCallNotPermitted(event -> {
                log.warn("Circuit Breaker [{}] Call not permitted - Circuit is OPEN", 
                    circuitBreaker.getName());
            });
    }
    
    private void sendAlert(String circuitName, String message) {
        // Integration with alerting system (Slack, PagerDuty, etc.)
        log.info("ALERT: Circuit [{}] - {}", circuitName, message);
    }
}
3.5 Controller with Circuit Breaker Endpoints
java
package com.example.resilience.controller;

import com.example.resilience.model.User;
import com.example.resilience.model.Order;
import com.example.resilience.model.Product;
import com.example.resilience.service.UserService;
import com.example.resilience.circuitbreaker.CircuitBreakerStateManager;
import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.CircuitBreaker;
import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.CircuitBreakerRegistry;
import lombok.RequiredArgsConstructor;
import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
import org.springframework.http.ResponseEntity;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.*;

import java.util.Map;
import java.util.concurrent.CompletableFuture;

@Slf4j
@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api")
@RequiredArgsConstructor
public class CircuitBreakerController {
    
    private final UserService userService;
    private final CircuitBreakerRegistry circuitBreakerRegistry;
    private final CircuitBreakerStateManager stateManager;
    
    @GetMapping("/users/{userId}")
    public ResponseEntity<User> getUser(@PathVariable String userId) {
        log.info("GET /users/{}", userId);
        User user = userService.getUser(userId);
        return ResponseEntity.ok(user);
    }
    
    @GetMapping("/orders/{orderId}")
    public ResponseEntity<CompletableFuture<Order>> getOrder(@PathVariable String orderId) {
        log.info("GET /orders/{}", orderId);
        CompletableFuture<Order> orderFuture = userService.getOrderWithProtection(orderId);
        return ResponseEntity.ok(orderFuture);
    }
    
    @GetMapping("/products/{productId}")
    public ResponseEntity<Product> getProduct(@PathVariable String productId,
                                              @RequestParam(defaultValue = "false") boolean critical) {
        log.info("GET /products/{}?critical={}", productId, critical);
        Product product = userService.getProduct(productId, critical);
        return ResponseEntity.ok(product);
    }
    
    /**
     * Get circuit breaker state for monitoring
     */
    @GetMapping("/circuit-breaker/state/{serviceName}")
    public ResponseEntity<Map<String, Object>> getCircuitBreakerState(
            @PathVariable String serviceName) {
        
        CircuitBreaker circuitBreaker = circuitBreakerRegistry
            .circuitBreaker(serviceName);
        
        if (circuitBreaker == null) {
            return ResponseEntity.notFound().build();
        }
        
        return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
            "name", circuitBreaker.getName(),
            "state", circuitBreaker.getState(),
            "metrics", circuitBreaker.getMetrics(),
            "failureRate", circuitBreaker.getMetrics().getFailureRate(),
            "slowCallRate", circuitBreaker.getMetrics().getSlowCallRate(),
            "totalCalls", circuitBreaker.getMetrics().getNumberOfSuccessfulCalls() + 
                          circuitBreaker.getMetrics().getNumberOfFailedCalls(),
            "successfulCalls", circuitBreaker.getMetrics().getNumberOfSuccessfulCalls(),
            "failedCalls", circuitBreaker.getMetrics().getNumberOfFailedCalls(),
            "notPermittedCalls", circuitBreaker.getMetrics().getNumberOfNotPermittedCalls()
        ));
    }
    
    /**
     * Get all circuit breaker states
     */
    @GetMapping("/circuit-breaker/states")
    public ResponseEntity<Map<String, Object>> getAllCircuitBreakerStates() {
        Map<String, Object> states = new java.util.HashMap<>();
        
        circuitBreakerRegistry.getAllCircuitBreakers().forEach(cb -> {
            states.put(cb.getName(), Map.of(
                "state", cb.getState(),
                "metrics", cb.getMetrics()
            ));
        });
        
        return ResponseEntity.ok(states);
    }
    
    /**
     * Reset circuit breaker (admin endpoint)
     */
    @PostMapping("/circuit-breaker/reset/{serviceName}")
    public ResponseEntity<Void> resetCircuitBreaker(@PathVariable String serviceName) {
        CircuitBreaker circuitBreaker = circuitBreakerRegistry
            .circuitBreaker(serviceName);
        
        if (circuitBreaker != null) {
            circuitBreaker.reset();
            log.info("Circuit breaker reset for service: {}", serviceName);
            return ResponseEntity.ok().build();
        }
        
        return ResponseEntity.notFound().build();
    }
    
    /**
     * Transition circuit breaker state manually for testing
     */
    @PostMapping("/circuit-breaker/transition/{serviceName}")
    public ResponseEntity<Void> transitionCircuitBreaker(
            @PathVariable String serviceName,
            @RequestParam CircuitBreaker.State targetState) {
        
        CircuitBreaker circuitBreaker = circuitBreakerRegistry
            .circuitBreaker(serviceName);
        
        if (circuitBreaker != null) {
            switch (targetState) {
                case OPEN:
                    circuitBreaker.transitionToOpenState();
                    break;
                case HALF_OPEN:
                    circuitBreaker.transitionToHalfOpenState();
                    break;
                case CLOSED:
                    circuitBreaker.transitionToClosedState();
                    break;
                case DISABLED:
                    circuitBreaker.transitionToDisabledState();
                    break;
                case FORCED_OPEN:
                    circuitBreaker.transitionToForcedOpenState();
                    break;
            }
            log.info("Circuit breaker {} transitioned to: {}", serviceName, targetState);
            return ResponseEntity.ok().build();
        }
        
        return ResponseEntity.notFound().build();
    }
    
    /**
     * Get detailed metrics including state transitions
     */
    @GetMapping("/circuit-breaker/metrics")
    public ResponseEntity<CircuitBreakerStateManager.CircuitBreakerMetrics> 
            getCircuitBreakerMetrics() {
        return ResponseEntity.ok(stateManager.getMetrics());
    }
}
4. Advanced Configuration & Customization
4.1 Custom Exception Handling
java
package com.example.resilience.exception;

import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.CircuitBreaker;
import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.CircuitBreakerConfig;
import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Component;

import java.util.Arrays;
import java.util.List;

@Slf4j
@Component
public class CustomExceptionHandler {
    
    /**
     * Custom record exception predicate for circuit breaker
     */
    public CircuitBreakerConfig.RecordExceptionPredicate createRecordExceptionPredicate() {
        return throwable -> {
            // Record HttpServerErrorException as failure
            if (throwable instanceof org.springframework.web.client.HttpServerErrorException) {
                log.debug("Recording HttpServerErrorException as failure");
                return true;
            }
            
            // Record TimeoutException as failure
            if (throwable instanceof java.util.concurrent.TimeoutException) {
                log.debug("Recording TimeoutException as failure");
                return true;
            }
            
            // Record IOException as failure
            if (throwable instanceof java.io.IOException) {
                log.debug("Recording IOException as failure");
                return true;
            }
            
            // Do NOT record BusinessException as failure
            if (throwable instanceof BusinessException) {
                log.debug("Ignoring BusinessException - not recording as failure");
                return false;
            }
            
            // Record all other exceptions
            log.debug("Recording unknown exception as failure: {}", throwable.getClass());
            return true;
        };
    }
    
    /**
     * Custom ignore exception predicate
     */
    public CircuitBreakerConfig.IgnoreExceptionPredicate createIgnoreExceptionPredicate() {
        return throwable -> {
            // Ignore validation exceptions
            if (throwable instanceof ValidationException) {
                log.debug("Ignoring ValidationException - circuit remains CLOSED");
                return true;
            }
            
            // Ignore rate limit exceptions
            if (throwable instanceof RateLimitException) {
                log.debug("Ignoring RateLimitException - circuit remains CLOSED");
                return true;
            }
            
            return false;
        };
    }
    
    /**
     * Exception classification for different fallback strategies
     */
    public enum ExceptionCategory {
        TRANSIENT,      // Temporary failures (retry)
        PERMANENT,      // Permanent failures (don't retry)
        BUSINESS,       // Business logic failures (ignore)
        UNKNOWN         // Unknown failures
    }
    
    public ExceptionCategory categorizeException(Throwable throwable) {
        if (throwable instanceof java.net.SocketTimeoutException ||
            throwable instanceof java.util.concurrent.TimeoutException) {
            return ExceptionCategory.TRANSIENT;
        }
        
        if (throwable instanceof BusinessException ||
            throwable instanceof ValidationException) {
            return ExceptionCategory.BUSINESS;
        }
        
        if (throwable instanceof org.springframework.web.client.HttpServerErrorException) {
            HttpServerErrorException ex = (HttpServerErrorException) throwable;
            if (ex.getStatusCode().is5xxServerError()) {
                return ExceptionCategory.TRANSIENT;
            }
            return ExceptionCategory.PERMANENT;
        }
        
        return ExceptionCategory.UNKNOWN;
    }
}

// Custom business exceptions
class BusinessException extends RuntimeException {
    public BusinessException(String message) {
        super(message);
    }
}

class ValidationException extends RuntimeException {
    public ValidationException(String message) {
        super(message);
    }
}

class RateLimitException extends RuntimeException {
    public RateLimitException(String message) {
        super(message);
    }
}
4.2 Dynamic Circuit Breaker Configuration
java
package com.example.resilience.config;

import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.CircuitBreaker;
import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.CircuitBreakerConfig;
import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.CircuitBreakerRegistry;
import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Component;

import java.time.Duration;
import java.util.concurrent.ConcurrentHashMap;

@Slf4j
@Component
public class DynamicCircuitBreakerManager {
    
    @Autowired
    private CircuitBreakerRegistry registry;
    
    private ConcurrentHashMap<String, CircuitBreakerConfig> dynamicConfigs = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();
    
    /**
     * Update circuit breaker configuration dynamically based on system load
     */
    public void updateConfigBasedOnLoad(String circuitName, SystemMetrics metrics) {
        CircuitBreaker circuitBreaker = registry.circuitBreaker(circuitName);
        
        if (circuitBreaker != null) {
            // Calculate new thresholds based on current load
            double failureThreshold = calculateDynamicFailureThreshold(metrics);
            int slidingWindowSize = calculateDynamicWindowSize(metrics);
            Duration waitDuration = calculateDynamicWaitDuration(metrics);
            
            CircuitBreakerConfig newConfig = CircuitBreakerConfig.custom()
                .failureRateThreshold(failureThreshold)
                .slidingWindowSize(slidingWindowSize)
                .waitDurationInOpenState(waitDuration)
                .permittedNumberOfCallsInHalfOpenState(calculateHalfOpenCalls(metrics))
                .build();
            
            // Replace configuration
            registry.replace(circuitName, newConfig);
            dynamicConfigs.put(circuitName, newConfig);
            
            log.info("Updated circuit breaker config for {}: failureThreshold={}, windowSize={}, waitDuration={}", 
                circuitName, failureThreshold, slidingWindowSize, waitDuration);
        }
    }
    
    private double calculateDynamicFailureThreshold(SystemMetrics metrics) {
        // Lower threshold when system is under high load
        if (metrics.getCpuUsage() > 0.8 || metrics.getMemoryUsage() > 0.9) {
            return 30.0; // More sensitive
        } else if (metrics.getErrorRate() > 0.1) {
            return 40.0; // Moderately sensitive
        }
        return 50.0; // Default
    }
    
    private int calculateDynamicWindowSize(SystemMetrics metrics) {
        // Larger window when system is stable
        if (metrics.getCpuUsage() < 0.5 && metrics.getErrorRate() < 0.05) {
            return 100; // More data for stable systems
        }
        return 20; // Smaller window for unstable systems
    }
    
    private Duration calculateDynamicWaitDuration(SystemMetrics metrics) {
        // Longer wait when errors are severe
        if (metrics.getErrorRate() > 0.3) {
            return Duration.ofSeconds(120);
        } else if (metrics.getErrorRate() > 0.15) {
            return Duration.ofSeconds(90);
        }
        return Duration.ofSeconds(60);
    }
    
    private int calculateHalfOpenCalls(SystemMetrics metrics) {
        // More test calls when system is stable
        if (metrics.getErrorRate() < 0.05) {
            return 10;
        }
        return 5;
    }
    
    @Data
    public static class SystemMetrics {
        private double cpuUsage;
        private double memoryUsage;
        private double errorRate;
        private int requestCount;
        private double averageResponseTime;
    }
}
4.3 Circuit Breaker with Fallback Cache
java
package com.example.resilience.cache;

import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.annotation.CircuitBreaker;
import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.cache.Cache;
import org.springframework.cache.CacheManager;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Component;

import java.util.concurrent.ConcurrentHashMap;
import java.util.concurrent.locks.ReentrantLock;

@Slf4j
@Component
public class CircuitBreakerCache {
    
    @Autowired
    private CacheManager cacheManager;
    
    private ConcurrentHashMap<String, ReentrantLock> locks = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();
    
    /**
     * Circuit breaker with cache-aside pattern
     */
    @CircuitBreaker(name = "data-service", fallbackMethod = "getFromCache")
    public Object getData(String key) {
        // Try primary source
        Object data = fetchFromPrimarySource(key);
        
        // Update cache
        updateCache(key, data);
        
        return data;
    }
    
    /**
     * Fallback that returns cached data
     */
    private Object getFromCache(String key, Exception ex) {
        log.warn("Circuit breaker triggered for key: {}, using cache fallback", key);
        
        // Try to get from cache
        Cache cache = cacheManager.getCache("data-cache");
        if (cache != null) {
            Cache.ValueWrapper wrapper = cache.get(key);
            if (wrapper != null) {
                return wrapper.get();
            }
        }
        
        // Cache miss - try to refresh synchronously with lock
        return refreshCacheSynchronously(key);
    }
    
    private Object refreshCacheSynchronously(String key) {
        ReentrantLock lock = locks.computeIfAbsent(key, k -> new ReentrantLock());
        
        lock.lock();
        try {
            // Double-check cache after acquiring lock
            Cache cache = cacheManager.getCache("data-cache");
            if (cache != null) {
                Cache.ValueWrapper wrapper = cache.get(key);
                if (wrapper != null) {
                    return wrapper.get();
                }
            }
            
            // Refresh from primary source
            Object data = fetchFromPrimarySource(key);
            updateCache(key, data);
            return data;
            
        } finally {
            lock.unlock();
        }
    }
    
    private Object fetchFromPrimarySource(String key) {
        // Simulate external service call
        log.info("Fetching data for key: {} from primary source", key);
        return "Data for " + key;
    }
    
    private void updateCache(String key, Object data) {
        Cache cache = cacheManager.getCache("data-cache");
        if (cache != null) {
            cache.put(key, data);
        }
    }
}
5. Monitoring & Metrics
5.1 Prometheus Metrics Configuration
java
package com.example.resilience.monitoring;

import io.micrometer.core.instrument.MeterRegistry;
import io.micrometer.core.instrument.Tags;
import io.micrometer.prometheus.PrometheusMeterRegistry;
import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.CircuitBreaker;
import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.CircuitBreakerRegistry;
import io.github.resilience4j.micrometer.tagged.CircuitBreakerMetrics;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.context.annotation.Bean;
import org.springframework.context.annotation.Configuration;
import org.springframework.scheduling.annotation.Scheduled;

@Configuration
public class MetricsConfig {
    
    @Autowired
    private MeterRegistry meterRegistry;
    
    @Autowired
    private CircuitBreakerRegistry circuitBreakerRegistry;
    
    @Bean
    public CircuitBreakerMetrics circuitBreakerMetrics() {
        return CircuitBreakerMetrics.ofCircuitBreakerRegistry(circuitBreakerRegistry);
    }
    
    @Scheduled(fixedDelay = 10000)
    public void recordCustomMetrics() {
        circuitBreakerRegistry.getAllCircuitBreakers().forEach(cb -> {
            // Record state as gauge
            meterRegistry.gauge("circuitbreaker.state",
                Tags.of("name", cb.getName()),
                cb,
                c -> c.getState().ordinal());
            
            // Record failure rate
            meterRegistry.gauge("circuitbreaker.failure.rate",
                Tags.of("name", cb.getName()),
                cb.getMetrics(),
                m -> m.getFailureRate() != null ? m.getFailureRate() : 0.0);
            
            // Record slow call rate
            meterRegistry.gauge("circuitbreaker.slowcall.rate",
                Tags.of("name", cb.getName()),
                cb.getMetrics(),
                m -> m.getSlowCallRate() != null ? m.getSlowCallRate() : 0.0);
            
            // Record buffered calls
            meterRegistry.gauge("circuitbreaker.buffered.calls",
                Tags.of("name", cb.getName()),
                cb.getMetrics(),
                m -> m.getNumberOfBufferedCalls());
            
            // Record successful calls
            meterRegistry.counter("circuitbreaker.calls.total",
                Tags.of("name", cb.getName(), "result", "success"),
                cb.getMetrics().getNumberOfSuccessfulCalls());
            
            // Record failed calls
            meterRegistry.counter("circuitbreaker.calls.total",
                Tags.of("name", cb.getName(), "result", "failure"),
                cb.getMetrics().getNumberOfFailedCalls());
            
            // Record not permitted calls (rejected)
            meterRegistry.counter("circuitbreaker.calls.total",
                Tags.of("name", cb.getName(), "result", "not_permitted"),
                cb.getMetrics().getNumberOfNotPermittedCalls());
        });
    }
}
5.2 Circuit Breaker Dashboard Endpoints
java
package com.example.resilience.controller;

import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.CircuitBreaker;
import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.CircuitBreakerRegistry;
import lombok.RequiredArgsConstructor;
import org.springframework.http.ResponseEntity;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.*;

import java.time.Instant;
import java.util.HashMap;
import java.util.Map;
import java.util.stream.Collectors;

@RestController
@RequestMapping("/admin/circuit-breaker")
@RequiredArgsConstructor
public class CircuitBreakerAdminController {
    
    private final CircuitBreakerRegistry circuitBreakerRegistry;
    
    @GetMapping("/dashboard")
    public ResponseEntity<Map<String, Object>> getDashboard() {
        Map<String, Object> dashboard = new HashMap<>();
        dashboard.put("timestamp", Instant.now());
        dashboard.put("circuit_breakers", circuitBreakerRegistry.getAllCircuitBreakers()
            .stream()
            .map(this::buildCircuitBreakerInfo)
            .collect(Collectors.toList()));
        
        return ResponseEntity.ok(dashboard);
    }
    
    @GetMapping("/{name}/details")
    public ResponseEntity<Map<String, Object>> getCircuitBreakerDetails(
            @PathVariable String name) {
        
        CircuitBreaker circuitBreaker = circuitBreakerRegistry.circuitBreaker(name);
        
        if (circuitBreaker == null) {
            return ResponseEntity.notFound().build();
        }
        
        return ResponseEntity.ok(buildCircuitBreakerInfo(circuitBreaker));
    }
    
    private Map<String, Object> buildCircuitBreakerInfo(CircuitBreaker cb) {
        Map<String, Object> info = new HashMap<>();
        CircuitBreaker.Metrics metrics = cb.getMetrics();
        
        info.put("name", cb.getName());
        info.put("state", cb.getState().toString());
        info.put("failure_rate", metrics.getFailureRate());
        info.put("slow_call_rate", metrics.getSlowCallRate());
        info.put("total_calls", metrics.getNumberOfSuccessfulCalls() + 
                                 metrics.getNumberOfFailedCalls());
        info.put("successful_calls", metrics.getNumberOfSuccessfulCalls());
        info.put("failed_calls", metrics.getNumberOfFailedCalls());
        info.put("slow_calls", metrics.getNumberOfSlowCalls());
        info.put("buffered_calls", metrics.getNumberOfBufferedCalls());
        info.put("not_permitted_calls", metrics.getNumberOfNotPermittedCalls());
        
        return info;
    }
}
6. Real-World Patterns
6.1 Chained Circuit Breakers Pattern
java
package com.example.resilience.pattern;

import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.annotation.CircuitBreaker;
import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Service;

@Slf4j
@Service
public class ChainedCircuitBreakerService {
    
    /**
     * Multiple circuit breakers in chain for different dependencies
     */
    @CircuitBreaker(name = "user-service")
    public User getUserWithDetails(String userId) {
        // Call user service
        User user = getUserFromService(userId);
        
        // Chain to order service circuit breaker
        return enrichWithOrders(user);
    }
    
    @CircuitBreaker(name = "order-service", fallbackMethod = "getOrdersFallback")
    private User enrichWithOrders(User user) {
        List<Order> orders = orderService.getOrders(user.getId());
        user.setOrders(orders);
        return user;
    }
    
    private User getOrdersFallback(User user, Exception ex) {
        log.warn("Order service circuit breaker open, returning user without orders");
        user.setOrders(Collections.emptyList());
        user.setOrdersStatus("UNAVAILABLE");
        return user;
    }
    
    /**
     * Dependency chain with cascading circuit breakers
     */
    @CircuitBreaker(name = "product-service")
    public Product getProductWithInventory(String productId) {
        Product product = productService.getProduct(productId);
        
        // Nested circuit breaker for inventory
        return enrichWithInventory(product);
    }
    
    @CircuitBreaker(name = "inventory-service", fallbackMethod = "inventoryFallback")
    private Product enrichWithInventory(Product product) {
        Inventory inventory = inventoryService.getInventory(product.getId());
        product.setStockQuantity(inventory.getQuantity());
        return product;
    }
    
    private Product inventoryFallback(Product product, Exception ex) {
        log.warn("Inventory service unavailable for product: {}", product.getId());
        product.setStockQuantity(-1); // Unknown stock
        product.setInventoryStatus("UNAVAILABLE");
        return product;
    }
}
6.2 Bulkhead with Circuit Breaker Pattern
java
package com.example.resilience.pattern;

import io.github.resilience4j.bulkhead.annotation.Bulkhead;
import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.annotation.CircuitBreaker;
import io.github.resilience4j.retry.annotation.Retry;
import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Service;

import java.util.concurrent.CompletableFuture;
import java.util.concurrent.ConcurrentHashMap;

@Slf4j
@Service
public class BulkheadCircuitBreakerPattern {
    
    // Thread pool for different service types
    private final ConcurrentHashMap<String, Integer> activeRequests = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();
    
    /**
     * Combined pattern: Circuit Breaker + Bulkhead + Retry
     * Prevents cascading failures and resource exhaustion
     */
    @CircuitBreaker(name = "critical-service")
    @Bulkhead(name = "critical-service-bulkhead", type = Bulkhead.Type.THREADPOOL)
    @Retry(name = "critical-service-retry")
    public CompletableFuture<Response> handleCriticalOperation(Request request) {
        log.info("Processing critical operation: {}", request.getId());
        
        // Track active requests
        activeRequests.merge(request.getId(), 1, Integer::sum);
        
        return CompletableFuture.supplyAsync(() -> {
            try {
                // Simulate processing
                Thread.sleep(1000);
                return new Response(request.getId(), "SUCCESS");
            } catch (InterruptedException e) {
                Thread.currentThread().interrupt();
                throw new RuntimeException(e);
            } finally {
                activeRequests.computeIfPresent(request.getId(), (k, v) -> v - 1);
            }
        });
    }
    
    /**
     * Get current bulkhead status
     */
    public Map<String, Object> getBulkheadStatus() {
        Map<String, Object> status = new HashMap<>();
        status.put("active_requests", activeRequests);
        status.put("active_request_count", activeRequests.values().stream().mapToInt(Integer::intValue).sum());
        return status;
    }
}
7. Testing & Validation
7.1 Unit Tests for Circuit Breaker
java
package com.example.resilience.test;

import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.CircuitBreaker;
import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.CircuitBreakerConfig;
import io.github.resilience4j.circuitbreaker.CircuitBreakerRegistry;
import org.junit.jupiter.api.BeforeEach;
import org.junit.jupiter.api.Test;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.boot.test.context.SpringBootTest;

import java.time.Duration;
import java.util.concurrent.TimeUnit;
import java.util.function.Supplier;

import static org.assertj.core.api.Assertions.assertThat;
import static org.assertj.core.api.Assertions.assertThatThrownBy;

@SpringBootTest
public class CircuitBreakerTest {
    
    @Autowired
    private CircuitBreakerRegistry registry;
    
    private CircuitBreaker circuitBreaker;
    
    @BeforeEach
    void setUp() {
        CircuitBreakerConfig config = CircuitBreakerConfig.custom()
            .failureRateThreshold(50)
            .slidingWindowSize(10)
            .minimumNumberOfCalls(5)
            .waitDurationInOpenState(Duration.ofSeconds(2))
            .build();
        
        circuitBreaker = CircuitBreaker.of("test-circuit", config);
    }
    
    @Test
    void testCircuitBreakerClosedState() {
        // Given - Circuit is closed
        assertThat(circuitBreaker.getState()).isEqualTo(CircuitBreaker.State.CLOSED);
        
        // When - Successful calls
        for (int i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
            circuitBreaker.executeSupplier(() -> "success");
        }
        
        // Then - Circuit remains closed
        assertThat(circuitBreaker.getState()).isEqualTo(CircuitBreaker.State.CLOSED);
        assertThat(circuitBreaker.getMetrics().getNumberOfSuccessfulCalls()).isEqualTo(5);
    }
    
    @Test
    void testCircuitBreakerOpensAfterFailures() {
        // Given - Circuit is closed
        assertThat(circuitBreaker.getState()).isEqualTo(CircuitBreaker.State.CLOSED);
        
        // When - 5 failures (threshold is 50% with 10 calls, but min 5 calls)
        for (int i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
            assertThatThrownBy(() -> 
                circuitBreaker.executeSupplier(() -> {
                    throw new RuntimeException("Service failure");
                }))
                .isInstanceOf(RuntimeException.class);
        }
        
        // After 5 failures and 5 successes, failure rate = 50%
        for (int i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
            circuitBreaker.executeSupplier(() -> "success");
        }
        
        // Then - Circuit should open after threshold exceeded
        assertThat(circuitBreaker.getState()).isEqualTo(CircuitBreaker.State.OPEN);
    }
    
    @Test
    void testCircuitBreakerHalfOpenTransition() throws InterruptedException {
        // Open the circuit
        openCircuit();
        
        // Wait for open state duration
        TimeUnit.SECONDS.sleep(3);
        
        // Circuit should transition to half-open
        assertThat(circuitBreaker.getState()).isEqualTo(CircuitBreaker.State.HALF_OPEN);
        
        // Execute successful call in half-open state
        String result = circuitBreaker.executeSupplier(() -> "success");
        
        // Circuit should close after successful call
        assertThat(result).isEqualTo("success");
        assertThat(circuitBreaker.getState()).isEqualTo(CircuitBreaker.State.CLOSED);
    }
    
    @Test
    void testCircuitBreakerRemainsOpenOnFailureInHalfOpen() throws InterruptedException {
        // Open the circuit
        openCircuit();
        
        // Wait for open state duration
        TimeUnit.SECONDS.sleep(3);
        
        // Circuit should transition to half-open
        assertThat(circuitBreaker.getState()).isEqualTo(CircuitBreaker.State.HALF_OPEN);
        
        // Execute failing call in half-open state
        assertThatThrownBy(() -> 
            circuitBreaker.executeSupplier(() -> {
                throw new RuntimeException("Service still failing");
            }))
            .isInstanceOf(RuntimeException.class);
        
        // Circuit should open again
        assertThat(circuitBreaker.getState()).isEqualTo(CircuitBreaker.State.OPEN);
    }
    
    private void openCircuit() {
        // Create failures to open circuit
        for (int i = 0; i < 6; i++) {
            try {
                circuitBreaker.executeSupplier(() -> {
                    throw new RuntimeException("Failure");
                });
            } catch (Exception e) {
                // Expected
            }
        }
        
        // Add successes to reach minimum calls
        for (int i = 0; i < 4; i++) {
            circuitBreaker.executeSupplier(() -> "success");
        }
        
        assertThat(circuitBreaker.getState()).isEqualTo(CircuitBreaker.State.OPEN);
    }
}
7.2 Integration Tests with WireMock
java
package com.example.resilience.test;

import com.github.tomakehurst.wiremock.WireMockServer;
import com.github.tomakehurst.wiremock.client.WireMock;
import org.junit.jupiter.api.AfterEach;
import org.junit.jupiter.api.BeforeEach;
import org.junit.jupiter.api.Test;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.boot.test.context.SpringBootTest;

import static com.github.tomakehurst.wiremock.client.WireMock.*;
import static org.assertj.core.api.Assertions.assertThat;

@SpringBootTest
public class CircuitBreakerIntegrationTest {
    
    @Autowired
    private UserService userService;
    
    private WireMockServer wireMockServer;
    
    @BeforeEach
    void setUp() {
        wireMockServer = new WireMockServer(8081);
        wireMockServer.start();
        WireMock.configureFor("localhost", 8081);
    }
    
    @AfterEach
    void tearDown() {
        wireMockServer.stop();
    }
    
    @Test
    void testCircuitBreakerOpensAfterMultipleFailures() {
        // Stub service to return 500 errors
        stubFor(get(urlPathMatching("/api/users/.*"))
            .willReturn(serverError()));
        
        // Make multiple calls to trigger circuit breaker
        for (int i = 0; i < 15; i++) {
            try {
                userService.getUser("test-" + i);
            } catch (Exception e) {
                // Expected
            }
        }
        
        // Circuit should be open now
        // Further calls should use fallback
        User user = userService.getUser("test");
        assertThat(user.getStatus()).isEqualTo("DEFAULT_FALLBACK");
    }
    
    @Test
    void testCircuitBreakerRecoversAfterSuccess() {
        // Initially return failures
        stubFor(get(urlPathEqualTo("/api/users/test"))
            .willReturn(serverError()));
        
        // Make calls to open circuit
        for (int i = 0; i < 10; i++) {
            try {
                userService.getUser("test");
            } catch (Exception e) {
                // Expected
            }
        }
        
        // Wait for half-open transition
        try {
            Thread.sleep(60000); // Wait for open state duration
        } catch (InterruptedException e) {
            Thread.currentThread().interrupt();
        }
        
        // Change stub to success
        stubFor(get(urlPathEqualTo("/api/users/test"))
            .willReturn(okJson("{\"id\":\"test\",\"name\":\"Test User\"}")));
        
        // Next call should succeed and close circuit
        User user = userService.getUser("test");
        assertThat(user).isNotNull();
        assertThat(user.getName()).isEqualTo("Test User");
    }
}
Summary
This comprehensive hands-on guide covers:

Circuit Breaker Fundamentals: Understanding the three states (CLOSED, OPEN, HALF-OPEN) and state transitions

Resilience4j Implementation: Complete Spring Boot integration with annotations and programmatic configuration

Advanced Patterns: Chained circuit breakers, bulkhead integration, and dynamic configuration

Monitoring: Prometheus metrics, actuator endpoints, and custom dashboards

Testing: Unit tests and integration tests with WireMock

Best Practices: Exception handling, fallback strategies, and configuration management

Key Takeaways:
Circuit breakers prevent cascading failures in distributed systems

Proper configuration of thresholds is critical for system stability

Combine with other patterns (retry, timeout, bulkhead) for comprehensive resilience

Monitor circuit breaker states and metrics for operational visibility

Test thoroughly with different failure scenarios

The circuit breaker pattern is essential for building resilient microservices that can gracefully handle failures and recover automatically.

Microservices Architecture:

Microservices are small business services that can work together and can be deployed autonomously / independently.

From Martin Fowlers Microservices article;

The microservice architectural style is an approach to developing a single application as a suite of small services, each running in its own process and communicating with lightweight mechanisms, often an HTTP or gRPC API.

So, we can say that Microservices Architecture is a cloud-native architectural approach in which applications are composed of many loosely coupled and independently deployable smaller components. Let´s describe some microservices characteristics:

They have their technology stack, including the database and data management model.
Communicate with each other over REST APIs, event streaming, and message brokers.
They are organized by business capability, with the line separating services, often referred to as a bounded context.
We will also see how we can decouple microservices with a bounded context in the upcoming sections.

Microservices Characteristics

Microservices are small, independent, and loosely coupled. A single small team of developers can write and maintain a service. Each service is a separate codebase, which a small development team can manage.

Those services can be deployed independently. A team can update an existing service without rebuilding and redeploying the entire application.

Each service is responsible for persisting its data or external state. This capability differs from the traditional model, where a separate data layer handles data persistence.

Benefits of Microservices Architecture

Agility — One of the essential characteristics of microservices is that the services are smaller and independently deployable.

Small, focused teams — A microservice should be small enough for a single feature team to build, test, and deploy.

Scalability — Microservices can be scaled independently, so you scale-out sub-services that require fewer resources without scaling out the entire application.

Challenges of Microservices Architecture
Complexity — The Microservices application has many services that need to work together and create value. Since there are many services, there are more moving parts than the monolithic application.

Network problems and latency — Since microservices are small and communicate with inter-service communication, we should manage network problems.

Data integrity — Each microservice has its own data persistence. So, data consistency can be a challenge.

Design Microservice Architecture:

Original Meaning:

Clients → Directly call Microservices → Each service has its own DB

Services:

Catalog Service
Shopping Cart Service
Discount Service
Ordering Service
Key Pattern:

👉 Database per Microservice

                   ┌──────────────────────┐
                   │     Client Apps      │
                   │  Web / Mobile / IoT  │
                   └─────────┬────────────┘
                             │
                    ┌────────▼────────┐
                    │   API Gateway   │
                    │ (Routing/Auth)  │
                    └────────┬────────┘
                             │
                ┌────────────▼────────────┐
                │   BFF Layer (Optional)  │
                └────────────┬────────────┘
                             │
     ┌───────────────┬───────────────┬───────────────┬───────────────┐
     ▼               ▼               ▼               ▼
┌─────────┐   ┌────────────┐   ┌──────────┐   ┌──────────┐
│ Catalog │   │ Shopping   │   │ Discount │   │ Ordering │
│ Service │   │ Cart       │   │ Service  │   │ Service  │
└────┬────┘   └────┬───────┘   └────┬─────┘   └────┬─────┘
     │             │               │              │
 ┌───▼───┐     ┌───▼───┐       ┌───▼───┐      ┌───▼───┐
 │  DB   │     │  DB   │       │  DB   │      │  DB   │
 └───────┘     └───────┘       └───────┘      └───────┘

                ┌────────────────────────────┐

                └────────────────────────────┘
				

We have followed the Database-per-Services Pattern when designing microservices architecture and put a database of each microservices. And microservices are decomposed from monolithic application modules with separated standalone services.

So now, these databases can be polyglot persistence. That means Product microservice can use the NoSQL document database, Shopping Cart microservice can use NoSQL key-value pair database, and Order microservice can use the Relational database following each microservices data storage requirements.

First Architectural Evolution:

Let’s look at the microservices architecture picture and consider what is missing from this architecture? What is the pain point of this architecture? How can we evolve this architecture to a better one to be more scalability, available, and able to accommodate more concurrent requests?		


See that Front end UI elements and Microservices communication are directly, and it seems complicated to manage all those connection points. Now we should focus on Microservices communications!

Microservices Communications:

Changing the communication mechanism is one of the biggest challenges when moving to a microservices-based application.

By nature, microservices are distributed; microservices communicate with each other by inter-service communication on the network level. Each microservice has its instance and process.

Therefore, services must interact using an inter-service communication protocol like HTTP, gRPC, or message brokers using the AMQP protocol.

Since microservices are complex structures into independently developed and deployed services, we should consider communication types and manage them into design phases.

Microservices Communication Design Patterns — API Gateway Pattern
The API gateway pattern is recommended if you want to design and build complex large microservices-based applications with multiple client applications.

The pattern provides a reverse proxy to redirect or route requests to your internal microservices endpoints. An API Gateway provides a single endpoint for the client applications and internally maps the requests to internal microservices. We should use API Gateways between client and internal microservices.

API Gateways can handle generic technical concerns like authorization, so instead of writing the same functionality in every microservices, authorization can be handled in a centralized way using the API Gateways and sent to internal microservices.

Also, the API Gateway manages routing to internal microservices and can aggregate several microservice requests in one response to the client.

In summary, the API Gateway will be placed between the client apps and the internal microservices working as a reverse proxy and routing client requests to backend services. It also provides generic technical concerns like authentication, SSL termination, and cache.
